### putting on hobnailed boots
 
 <i>phr.</i>

>  <b>HOCKEN</b> A fortnight before its sittings, Mr. Peter Proudfoot, Chief Commissioner of Lands, died, and the Superintendent appointed his own son-in-law, Mr. Cutten, to the vacant post, which was in the gift of the Executive and should have been filled by them. In his opening speech to the Council he justified this action, or rather rode roughshod over any objections to it. `Now,' he said, `I would have Mr. Cutten and all others to know that I am in no degree to be swayed from doing what I have a right to do - to put the right man into the right place - whether that man happens to be my son-in-law or any one else' - a mode of speech now known amongst politicians as `putting on hobnailed boots.' <i>(1898,Contributions to the Early History of New Zealand,181</i>)

### PWD
 
 <i>abbr.</i> Public Works Department; former abbrev. and short name [pi:d^bju:di:] for the Public Works Department; thence MOW (Ministry of Works) now (1984) MWD (Ministry of Works and Development. express: to work on the PWD.

>  Speaking of the Treaty we were very nearly breaking one of its provisions, that which related to parumoana or mud flats, ... It arose out of a proposal of the Unemployment Board, backed by P.W.D., Lands and Marine, to reclaim mudflats at Hokianga, Whangarei and Whangaroa. <i>(May 30, 1932,Na To Hoa Aroha,2/273,1987 ed.</i>) [NOTES: (Ngata)]

>  <i>(1944,Whitcombe's Modern Junior Dictionary (8th edn),454</i>)

>  <b>SLATTER</b> <i>(1959,Gun in My Hand, A,10</i>) [NOTES: [see quot cup]]

>  <b>BAILEY & ROTH</b> I recall the lone days in the ranges,/ In camps of the P.W.D. <i>(1967,Shanties,137</i>) [NOTES: 1962 Aug <i>Fernfire</i> No 9 in 1967 Bailey and Roth Shanties 137]

>  <b>ANDERSON</b> ... I told my little friend that I was going to a job in the Public Works Department (PWD) in Southland ... . He then told me of a [3] few PWD paymasters. <i>Ibid.</i> 95 In the PWD there was a sprinkling of what were known as `compo kings'. These were men who always seemed to be on compo [either by bad luck, accident prone-ness, or malingering]. <i>(1975,Milford Road,2</i>)

>  <b>GRADY</b> In a rather roundabout way, it was the PWD who were instrumental in bringing Trevor back to harpooning duties in war-time. <i>(1982,Perano Whalers,86</i>) [NOTES: [reference 1943]]

>  <b>MIDDLETON</b> I'll see that P.W.D. joker next week. <i>(1964,Walk on the Beach,172</i>) [NOTES: (Not for a Seagull)]



 
 <i>abbr.</i> [attrib. uses]

>  <b>SLATTER</b> Juvenile delinquency. What about us? ... [10] ... Bowling the P.W.D. wheelbarrows over the bank and down the hill. <i>(1959,Gun in My Hand, A,9</i>)

>  <b>SUTHERLAND</b> For a number of married men there were transfers to other P.W.D. jobs. <i>(1956,Green Kiwi,210,1960 ed.</i>)

>  <b>GRADY</b> So too were some of the tougher elements of the PWD (Public Works Department) men, who took two years to build the Whekenui gun emplacement [in 1943]. <i>(1982,Perano Whalers,85</i>)

>  <b>RICHARDS</b> The men lived in the Public Works Department (P.W.D.) tents which were supplied with a wooden floor and tin fireplace. <i>(1986,Off the Sheep's Back,105</i>)

>  <b>HENDERSON</b> Cosmos the comforter of a thousand raw huts in raw clay in raw seacoast or raw inland valley on godforsaken PWD and Railway and Hydro projects. <i>(1981,Exiles of Asbestos Cottage, The,86</i>)

### pygmy
 
 <i>adj.</i> (See [mistletoe](mar-moa.md#mistletoe),[pine](per-pop.md#pine))

### pygmy pine
 
 <i>n.</i> a sprawling shrub related to the rimu [aka dwarf-pine, dwarf pine; <i>Lepidothamnus laxifolius</i> previously <i>Dacryidium laxifolius</i>]

>  New Zealand's wet alpine areas are home to what is thought to be the world's smallest conifer, the pygmy pine, Lepidothamnus laxifolius. This "pine" doesn't even make it as a shrub, let alone a forest specimen, but spreads in a cushion. <i>(Aug 25, 2001,Dominion Weekend Magazine,10</i>)

>  <i>Dacrydium laxifolium</i> pigmy pine <i>(1908,AJHR</i>) [NOTES: (And) Cockayne Report on a Botanical Survey of the Tongariro National Park]

>  <i>Dacrydium laxifolium</i> pigmy pine <i>(1909,AJHR</i>) [NOTES: Cockayne Report on a Botanical Survey of Stewart Island (And)]

>  <b>COCKAYNE</b> <i>(1919,NZ Plants & Their Story</i>) [NOTES: (And)]

>  <b>COCKAYNE</b> <i>(1924,Cultivation of NZ Plants, The</i>) [NOTES: (And)]

>  <b>LAING & BLACKWELL</b> <i>Dacrydium laxifolium (The Loose-leaved Dacrydium)</i>. ... Pigmy pine. <i>(1940,Plants of NZ,77</i>)

>  <b>HUNT</b> It (New Zealand) has the largest tree in the world in the Kauri ... and the smallest pine in the world, the Pygmy Pine (<i>Dacrydium laxifolium</i> ), of sub-alpine areas, the height of which is normally about twelve inches ... <i>(1951,Confessions,136</i>)

>  <b>MARTIN</b> To this genus belong the PYGMY PINE (<i>D[acrydium] laxifolium</i>), the smallest conifer in the world. <i>(1961,Flora NZ,176</i>)

>  <b>MARK & ADAMS</b> Small trailing shrubs - the pygmy pine (<i>Dacrydium laxifolium</i> ), creeping heath plants -dracophyllums (<i>D. prostratum, D. politum), Pentachondra pumila, Cyathodes pumila </i>and <i>C. empetrifolia,</i> also <i>Coprosma pumila</i> are typical of peat bogs. <i>(1973,NZ Alpine Plants,22</i>)

>  <b>WILSON</b> <i>Dacrydium laxifolium</i> PYGMY PINE ...Sprawling shrub with long, flexible branches. ... <i>(1982,Stewart Island Plants,48</i>)

>  <b>DAWSON</b> The pigmy pine (<i>Lepidothamnus laxifolius</i>), sometimes called the smallest conifer in the world, forms even lower mat-like patches in poorly drained sites ... <i>(1988,Forest Vines to Snow Tussocks,170</i>)

>  The New Zealand pygmy weed, taken to Britain as an aquarium plant, has recently been discovered invading waterways there. <i>(Nov 3, 2003,Dominion Post,B5</i>)

### pyjams
 
 <i>n.</i> pyjamas [1984 July p.c. Bill Griffiths (Pakuranga College) Also besides `Jarmies' childish for `pajamas' [Macquarie] could have two common NZ expressions `PJ's' and `pyjams'.]

### PYM
 
 <i>abbr.</i> Progressive Youth Movement, a Communist-allied youth group

>  <b>McG</b> And dogs, madam, dings dallies dutchies dagoes boongs chinks gooks wogs commies coconuts coons kaffirs pommies polys woollys gollys spics spucs spastics the PYM queers and other long-haired fairies - yes! We love them all! <i>(1981,Foreskin's Lament,40</i>)

>  <b>SHADBOLT</b> That the PYM are anarchists. That criminals are bad people. That China is poised on the coasts and preparing to land on our beaches. <i>(1971,Bullshit and Jellybeans,89</i>)

### PYO
 
 <i>abbr.</i> pick your own (fruit or vegetables) [1987 Jan p.c. W.S. Ramson and Joan Hughes: seen as a sign outside Otaki coast berry farms Jim writes : In NZPOD and AMO but not in any other dictionary I have seen.] (See [cf. BYO](bri-byo.md#byo))

>  [Advt] Su's / Gardens / PYO / Tomato 50c kg / PYO. <i>(Feb 17, 2001,Hawke's Bay Today,29</i>)

>  MANZ Brothers Gardens open for PYO sweetcorn ... PEAS PYO, 70 kg. quantity discount ... RASPBERRIES. Packed or PYO. <i>(Jan 8, 1987,Evening Post,23</i>)
